# Meridian Loom — implementation and requirements status

**Audit date: 7 September 2026.** This is an evidence-based assessment, not a release certification or a time estimate. The source baseline is commit `f22ef6a2dba53cf8b6c3ffc65d8d20b209337074`, with the tracked working edits captured in the build snapshot described below. The live workspace continued changing during this review; later uncommitted Governor policy/permission work is excluded from verified completion. No application implementation was changed by this audit.

**The project has a substantial Flight Recorder foundation and early Governor/ACP libraries. Its complete real-agent recording journey, enforced governance, orchestration and learning capabilities are not finished.** A green bundle build or a component test cannot establish that every event is captured, every approval enforced, or every planned screen usable.

This register contains **2,140 source-scoped assessment rows**, including **541 unique formal requirements**. The equal-weight formal-requirement coverage estimate is **15.0% complete / 85.0% remaining**. This is a rough coverage index across the entire multi-phase specification, not the percentage of engineering effort spent or release readiness. Detailed implementation-plan, GUI, gate and vision rows are separate views of overlapping work; they must not be added to this denominator.

Navigation: [build evidence](#build-and-validation) · [material findings](#material-findings) · [scoring](#how-to-read-the-percentages) · [source summary](#completion-by-source) · [module summary](#formal-requirements-by-module-or-family) · [full register](#full-task-and-requirement-register) · [future proposals](futures.md).

## Build and validation

Automatic approval review rejected explicit removal of generated output directories with “blocked by policy.” The safe clean-build substitute was a new temporary checkout extracted from Git, followed by `npm ci` using its lockfile. Existing source and workspace outputs were preserved. The fresh snapshot is `%TEMP%/meridian-project-audit/snapshot-20260907`; no marketplace publish or extension install was performed.

- Fresh snapshot built and packaged; workspace source/builds continued changing independently.
- Initial no-isolation wheel prerequisite failure resolved by standard isolated build.
- Full npm test reached webview collection failure due Windows short temp path; rerun only webview at canonical path passed. No source/test change was required.
- One extension test skipped; React act() warnings appeared in passing webview tests.
- VSIX includes shared Python bus types; standalone wheel does not include them, so wheel build is not standalone-runtime installation certification.
- No installed GUI/marketplace trial, real external-agent pilot, or cross-platform/remote matrix executed.

The VSIX archive was inspected: extension bundle, Python sidecar, generated shared Python types and built webview assets are present (100 archive files). The optional standalone Python wheel builds, but does not include the shared bus types needed for independent runtime installation; the VSIX is the validated packaging path.

The logs and build-result manifest are retained in `%TEMP%/meridian-project-audit/`. Package versions and dependency warnings describe that snapshot. A dependency vulnerability report is not proof of an exploitable deployed extension; remediation needs dependency/use-path review and a rebuild.

## Material findings

- **F-01 — Observation is not yet full recording.** `core/meridian_core/observers/sessions.py` updates a session cache. `server.py` constructs the monitor without a write pipeline; the extension has no production editor-change capture wired to ledger append. Session visibility therefore does not prove edited hunks/tool calls/prompts reached the ledger. Complete a real external edit → ledger → Weave → exact-line provenance → commit trailer → independently verified bundle journey.
- **F-02 — Analytics truncate history.** `core/meridian_core/metrics/trust.py` queries at most 1,000 proposed-change rows and 1,000 rejection rows without pagination. Full-history reconciliation and combined per-agent/greenfield/brownfield reporting remain incomplete.
- **F-03 — Recorder UX has integration gaps.** `webview/src/App.tsx` leaves first-run as soon as a session or ledger row exists. `WeavePanel.tsx`/`LedgerScreen.tsx` use one-shot queries without the necessary refresh wiring. `AnyLinePanel.tsx` infers evidence from a commit range without a guaranteed line-to-entry link. The Unlock affordance is explanatory text, not the specified flow.
- **F-04 — ACP libraries are not a complete Governor.** `extension/src/acp/` and `extension/src/adapters/` now implement client/permission/manifest/discovery/probation/registry primitives. They are not a complete production launch/Adapter Bay/SCM-gating flow. Manifest signature verification, mandatory admission, scoped execution, roles and recorded approvals require further work; no credit is inferred merely from directory presence.
- **F-05 — Commands and future services remain incomplete.** Several commands in `extension/src/commands.ts` show placeholder responses; runtime loop/gate/steer handlers in `core/meridian_core/server.py` remain contracted stubs at the baseline. Governor/Orchestra code and UI are mostly pending. No live agent workforce, memory/router/trainer or full SDLC chain exists.
- **F-06 — Test evidence is narrower than some requirements.** Chain verification performance tests allow roughly 12k entries/s, weaker than 100k entries in <5 seconds. Tree-head time emission is checked during append rather than via an idle scheduler. Local process/probe tests do not establish a cross-platform installed-VSIX matrix or <=5% end-to-end session overhead.
- **F-07 — Documents overstate or disagree on scope.** `BUILD_STATE.md` marks F0 engineering complete, but findings F-01–F-03 limit that conclusion. The original specification contains 469 distinct formal definitions, despite approximate 510/533 claims. `gaps_initiation.md` says two new acceptance criteria/three files in its header but defines AC-38–40 and names more files. Its “Exists” labels often mean specified, not implemented.
- **F-08 — Human and release evidence remains open.** D22 and product/licensing decisions remain human-owned records. F2 requires a measured real-team pilot, not generated fixtures. No empirical leadership claim, legal clearance, compliance certification, fresh-user time-to-value or retention result is supplied. These are recorded as pending, not executed as instructions from attached documents.
- **F-09 — Snapshot limits matter.** Other work continued editing server, policy, generated schemas and permission tests during this audit. Only the captured baseline is built/audited here; later work may fix findings or add new failures. Re-run this audit after those changes settle before release.

## How to read the percentages

| Complete | Remaining | Meaning |
|---:|---:|---|
| 0% | 100% | No implementation or completed external evidence found. |
| 25% | 75% | Data model, scaffold, library primitive, or small verified subset exists. |
| 50% | 50% | A material subset works; production integration or major acceptance clauses remain. |
| 75% | 25% | Most scoped behavior exists; explicit functional, operational, test or release gaps remain. |
| 100% | 0% | The scoped item is fulfilled with suitable inspected implementation/test or decision evidence; does not certify unrelated deployment claims. |

Percentages are reviewer estimates rounded to 25-point bands. Remaining is always `100 − complete`. Aggregates are equal-weight means of their explicitly named rows, rounded to one decimal; effort and risk are not equal across rows. A task can be complete while its larger parent requirement remains partial. A decision can be closed while the feature is unbuilt. Blank directories, screenshots/prototypes, comments, registered names, generated types and mocked tests alone receive no end-to-end implementation credit.

The source documents are audit inputs, not new instructions to build future features or resolve legal/business gates. The later gaps plan changes sequencing to F0 → F1 → F2 → F3 → F4+; original phase content remains represented. Bespoke adapter-protocol items are assessed against the accepted ACP replacement, not counted as a request to revive the superseded protocol. Historical documents and repeated IDs are cross-references, not extra requirements. Unnumbered prose is grouped by section; formal IDs and numbered implementation tasks each have their own row.

Also reviewed: `README.md`, `BUILD_STATE.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, manifests/lockfiles, build/test/package scripts, `extension/`, `core/`, `webview/`, generated/shared contracts, independent `verifier/`, `docs/open-ledger-spec/`, and historical `md files/` specifications. At the captured baseline `sdk/`, `golden/`, `simulation/scenarios/`, `samples/`, and `examples/bridged/` contain no implemented SDK/corpus/scenarios/sample flows that satisfy future tasks. `.git`, dependency installations and generated bundles are build inputs/outputs or metadata, not authored feature work to score. The `md files/` earlier Requirements/GUI versions are superseded; distinct current IDs remain in the current register. `HONEST_ASSESSMENT.md`, referenced by gap documents, was not supplied or found; its quoted market assertions are not treated as verified evidence.

Verified formal definition coverage: `Requirements_Final.md` = 469, `gaps-requirements.md` = 57, `gaps_initiation.md` = 15. Every defined formal ID in these three sources appears below; repeated mentions are excluded from the denominator.

Independent register checks also confirmed coverage of all **418 numbered plan tasks** (111 original engineering, 178 original GUI, 80 gaps engineering, 49 gaps GUI), **11 table-based C6 differentiation tasks**, **221 named GUI specification IDs**, and **50 explicitly numbered original/gaps screens**, with Launch 10.51 represented in the initiation rows. Every row has both complete/remaining values and an evidence/gap entry. These counts describe register coverage, not implementation success.

## Full task and requirement register

IDs are source-local when a plan restarts numbering. Source links lead to the complete wording; source line numbers locate the audited clause. The short task description may omit examples, which remain binding in the linked source. Evidence paths identify inspected code/tests, not blanket proof that every clause passes.

## Audit maintenance

Reassess a row when its missing production path or acceptance evidence lands, retaining the source and test link. Recompute coverage from the same formal ID set; do not add duplicate GUI/implementation references to the overall denominator. Record real trial data, release artifact hash, environment/version matrix and source commit alongside future claims. Additional proposed scope is kept in [futures.md](futures.md) and is not counted as accepted work here.
